Please conduct a strict technical code review on the implementation of the AI PPT Two-Pass Review Workflow.

1. `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/prompts/25_slide_review.md`
2. `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/adapters/ai_adapter.py`
3. `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py`

## Review Focus & Criteria:

   - Does `api_generate` and `generate_live` in `app.py` correctly handle the two-pass generation when `review=True`?
   - Are errors in the review pass handled gracefully (falling back to initial draft without breaking)?

Provide a clear, detailed code review report covering:
- **Summary Verdict**: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: BLOCK` (with reasons)
- **Detailed Evaluation**: Itemized feedback for Scope 1 and Scope 2.
- **Recommendations & Edge Cases**: Any potential issues or edge cases found.

===== tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\prompts\25_slide_review.md =====
# Stage 2.5 ??Slide Review & Refinement

You are an expert presentation reviewer for Vieworks executive presentations.
Your goal is to critique and refine a drafted slide IR (`canvas-ir/1`), improving formatting, conciseness, visual hierarchy, and ensuring ZERO text overflow.

## Slide to Review
```json
{{DRAFT_SLIDE_IR}}
```

## Target Layout Budget & Rules
- Layout ID: `{{LAYOUT_ID}}`
- Semantic Role: `{{SEMANTIC_ROLE}}`
{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}

## Review Objectives
1. **Zero Overflow**: Ensure text does not exceed the line and bullet limits defined in the budget. Shorten sentences or bullet points if necessary.
2. **Impact & Formatting**: Ensure clear, concise professional phrasing. Use proper bold/italic formatting where appropriate to highlight key metrics or terms.
3. **Schema Adherence**: Output MUST strictly conform to the `slide` object schema. Do not change `layout_id` or `id`.

## Output Instruction
Return ONLY a valid JSON object corresponding to the refined slide. No explanation, no markdown code fences.

===== tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\src\aippt\adapters\ai_adapter.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""LLM  ->  Slide IR.  Model-agnostic by construction.

STATUS: the generative pipeline (``build_system_prompt``, ``generate_outline``,
``fill_slide``, ``generate_deck``) and ``CliWorkerClient`` (headless claude/codex CLI,
no API key -- see the headless-worker-integration Work Order) are implemented. The
reference API-key clients below (``ClaudeClient``, ``OpenAIClient``, ``GeminiClient``)
still raise NotImplementedError -- out of scope; the user declined that path.

The single invariant this module exists to protect
[REDACTED]
**The model never emits PPTX, OOXML, DrawingML, coordinates, fonts or colours.**
It emits schema-valid IR: a ``layout_id`` plus placeholder content. Everything visual
comes from the template and the renderer. If you ever find yourself adding an `x`, `y`,
`rgb` or `font_size` field to the IR to make a slide look right, the bug is in the
renderer or the template -- fix it there.

Why structured output and not free-text-then-parse
[REDACTED]
"Return JSON" in a system prompt yields JSON *most* of the time, and the failures are
the expensive kind: a stray markdown fence, a trailing comma, a hallucinated field. Every
supported provider can constrain output to a schema, so we use that and get a hard
guarantee instead of a soft one:

    Claude  -> tool use / `tools=[{input_schema: SCHEMA}]`, force with tool_choice
    OpenAI  -> Structured Outputs / `response_format={type: json_schema, strict: true}`
    Gemini  -> `response_mime_type="application/json"` + `response_schema`

All three consume the *same* ``ir/schema.json``. That is the entire point of having an IR:
the schema is the neutral contract, and the model behind it is a swappable implementation
detail. Adding a provider must not require touching the renderer, the prompts, or the IR.

Two-stage generation (see prompts/)
[REDACTED]
    stage 1  10_outline_storyboard.md  topic + sources -> deck narrative, one line/slide
    stage 2  20_slide_fill.md          one slide's outline entry -> that slide's IR

Stage 2 runs per-slide and is parallelisable. The split exists because a single call that
has to hold the whole deck's narrative *and* respect 30 different per-placeholder
character budgets does neither job well: it drifts on structure and overflows on length.
Separating "what story do we tell" from "what words go in this box" fixes both.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import math
import os
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Protocol

from jsonschema import validators
from jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError

from ..ir.models import (
    Bullets,
    Callout,
    Cards,
    Deck,
    DeckMeta,
    Heading,
    IR_VERSION,
    IRValidationError,
    Paragraph,
    Slide,
    Steps,
    Table,
    load_schema,
)

#: This module's project root -- ``tasks/AI_Powered_PPT`` -- three levels above
#: ``src/aippt/adapters/ai_adapter.py``. Same convention ``canvas/pin.py`` and
#: ``tools/calibrate.py`` use for the same directory depth. ``prompts/`` and
#: ``reference/`` are data files, not package resources: the repo-root ``.gitignore``
#: keeps them out of a fresh checkout of ``tasks/*``, so this resolves correctly in a
#: full working copy but not inside a staging clone that only carries ``src/``.
_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
_PROMPTS_DIR = _PROJECT_ROOT / "prompts"
_REFERENCE_DIR = _PROJECT_ROOT / "reference"

#: Where ``_run_cli`` mirrors a worker subprocess's live stderr, and where ``app.py``'s
#: ``GET /api/log`` reads it back from -- the same ``_PROJECT_ROOT`` convention as
#: ``_PROMPTS_DIR``/``_REFERENCE_DIR`` above, so both modules agree on the path without
#: either hardcoding the other's.
_ARTIFACTS_DIR = _PROJECT_ROOT / "artifacts"
_LIVE_LOG_PATH = _ARTIFACTS_DIR / "multiagent_live.log"


class LLMClient(Protocol):
    """The seam. Claude, Codex and Gemini are interchangeable *behind this*.

    One method, because one method is all the pipeline needs. Anything provider-specific
    (auth, retries, rate limits, base URLs) lives inside the implementation, never leaks
    into the caller.
    """

    def complete_json(
        self,
        *,
        system: str,
        user: str,
        schema: dict[str, Any],
        max_tokens: int = 8000,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Return a dict the provider has *already* constrained to ``schema``.

        Implementations MUST use the provider's native structured-output mechanism.
        An implementation that calls ``json.loads`` on free text is not conforming --
        it silently reintroduces exactly the failure mode this interface removes.
        """
        ...


@dataclass
class GenerationResult:
    deck: Deck
    attempts: int
    repaired: bool          # True if a retry was needed -- track this; a rising rate
                            # means the prompt or the budget has drifted from reality.
    token_usage: dict[str, int]


def build_system_prompt(manifest: dict[str, Any], catalog_md: str) -> str:
    """Assemble the system prompt from ``prompts/00_system_deck_author.md`` + the catalog.

    The catalog (``reference/layout_catalog.md``) is injected *whole*. It carries the two
    things the model cannot guess and must not invent:

      * the exact set of legal ``layout_id`` values, and
      * the per-placeholder **content budget** ("max 6 bullets x 9 words", "title <= 42
        Korean chars").

    Injecting the budget is what makes the model self-limit. Without it the model writes
    to a length that reads well in chat and overflows a 4-inch box every time -- and since
    python-pptx cannot autofit, that overflow is permanent and invisible until render.

    Note the budget is stated in BOTH Latin and Korean character counts, because a Hangul
    glyph is roughly twice the advance width of a Latin one. A model told "60 characters"
    with no script qualifier will happily overflow a Korean slide by 2x.
    """
    del manifest  # the caller already rendered catalog_md from this manifest; see _load_catalog_md
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "00_system_deck_author.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    return template.replace("{{LAYOUT_CATALOG}}", catalog_md)


def _load_catalog_md() -> str:
    """The pre-rendered ``reference/layout_catalog.md`` -- read from disk rather than
    regenerated via ``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog(manifest)``, so this module never
    needs a ``python-pptx`` dependency of its own."""
    return (_REFERENCE_DIR / "layout_catalog.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")


def _outline_schema(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The stage-1 structured-output schema. ``layout_id`` is a closed ``enum`` built from
    the manifest's own layouts -- an invented layout is rejected by the provider's own
    structured-output mechanism, before it ever reaches ``validate_against_manifest``.
    """
    layout_ids = sorted({
        lay["layout_id"]
        for master in manifest.get("masters", [])
        for lay in master.get("layouts", [])
    })
    slide_entry = {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["id", "layout_id", "title", "intent", "key_points"],
        "additionalProperties": False,
        "properties": {
            "id": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
            "layout_id": {"type": "string", "enum": layout_ids},
            "title": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 200},
            "intent": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
            "key_points": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
            "source_refs": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
        },
    }
    return {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["slides", "rationale"],
        "additionalProperties": False,
        "properties": {
            "slides": {"type": "array", "minItems": 1, "items": slide_entry},
            "rationale": {"type": "string"},
        },
    }


def generate_outline(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
                     manifest: dict[str, Any], target_slides: int = 12) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Stage 1: topic + source material -> a storyboard.

    Returns one entry per slide::

        {"id": "slide-03", "layout_id": "...", "title": "...",
         "intent": "why this slide exists, one line",
         "key_points": ["...", "..."]}

    The outline is deliberately NOT full IR: no blocks, no prose. Its job is narrative
    arc and layout selection -- deciding that slide 3 is a section divider and slide 4 is
    a comparison table. Cheap to generate, cheap to review, cheap to regenerate.

    ``layout_id`` is chosen here, at deck level, where the model can see the whole shape
    and avoid emitting nine consecutive bullet slides.
    """
    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "10_outline_storyboard.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    user_prompt = (
        template.replace("{{TOPIC}}", topic)
                .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
                .replace("{{TARGET_SLIDES}}", str(target_slides))
    )
    result = client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt,
                                  schema=_outline_schema(manifest))
    return result["slides"]


def _find_layout(manifest: dict[str, Any], layout_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """``layout_id`` -> its full manifest entry. Raises plain ``CliWorkerError`` (not
    ``CliWorkerSchemaError``) when the name doesn't exist at all: that is a structural
    defect no repair prompt can fix, since the outline schema's own ``enum`` should have
    made it unreachable in the first place.
    """
    for master in manifest.get("masters", []):
        for lay in master.get("layouts", []):
            if lay["layout_id"] == layout_id:
                return lay
    raise CliWorkerError(
        f"layout_id {layout_id!r} does not exist in this manifest's layouts -- "
        "no repair prompt can fix a layout the template does not have."
    )


def _format_layout_budget(layout: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """One layout's placeholder budgets, in the same per-placeholder shape
    ``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog`` uses for the full catalog -- but scoped to just
    THIS layout, which is the point of stage 2's narrower prompt (see module docstring).
    """
    lines = [f"Layout `{layout['layout_id']}` ({layout['semantic_role']}):"]
    placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
    if not placeholders:
        lines.append("- this layout has no fillable placeholders (decoration only)")
        return "\n".join(lines)
    for ph in placeholders:
        b = ph.get("content_budget") or {}
        head = f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph['type']}"
        if not b.get("computable"):
            lines.append(f"{head} -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason')})")
        elif "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
            lines.append(
                f"{head} -- max {b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin / "
                f"{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars, <= {b['recommended_lines']} lines"
            )
        elif "max_bullets" in b:
            lines.append(
                f"{head} -- max {b['max_bullets']} bullets x {b['max_words_per_bullet']} "
                f"words ({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
                f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)"
            )
        else:
            lines.append(f"{head} -- {b.get('chars_per_line_latin')} latin chars/line, "
                         f"{b.get('max_lines')} lines")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _slide_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """``ir/schema.json``'s ``$defs.slide`` lifted to the document root, with the full
    ``$defs`` carried alongside so its internal ``#/$defs/...`` refs still resolve -- a
    JSON Schema ``$ref`` is resolved against the root of whatever document is actually
    being validated, and stage 2 validates one slide, not a whole deck.
    """
    full = load_schema()
    schema = dict(full["$defs"]["slide"])
    schema["$schema"] = full.get("$schema", "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema")
    schema["$defs"] = full["$defs"]
    return schema


def fill_slide(client: LLMClient, outline_entry: dict[str, Any],
               manifest: dict[str, Any], sources: list[str], *,
               repair_note: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Stage 2: one outline entry -> one schema-valid slide object.

    The prompt injects ONLY the budget for the layout this slide actually uses -- not the
    whole catalog. Narrowing the context measurably improves adherence: the model is not
    picking a layout any more, it is filling a box whose exact dimensions it has been told.

    ``repair_note``, keyword-only and additive to the original signature: when
    ``generate_deck``'s bounded repair loop re-prompts after a validation failure, the
    specific error goes here and is substituted into ``{{REPAIR_NOTE}}``.
    """
    layout = _find_layout(manifest, outline_entry["layout_id"])
    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "20_slide_fill.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    repair_block = (
        f"## This slide was rejected -- fix it\n\n{repair_note}" if repair_note else ""
    )
    user_prompt = (
        template.replace("{{OUTLINE_ENTRY}}",
                         json.dumps(outline_entry, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))
                .replace("{{LAYOUT_ID}}", layout["layout_id"])
                .replace("{{SEMANTIC_ROLE}}", layout["semantic_role"])
                .replace("{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}", _format_layout_budget(layout))
                .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
                .replace("{{REPAIR_NOTE}}", repair_block)
    )
    return client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt, schema=_slide_schema())


def review_slide(reviewer_client: LLMClient, slide_dict: dict[str, Any],
                 manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Stage 2.5: Review and refine a filled slide object using a secondary reviewer model."""
    layout = _find_layout(manifest, slide_dict["layout_id"])
    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "25_slide_review.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    user_prompt = (
        template.replace("{{DRAFT_SLIDE_IR}}", json.dumps(slide_dict, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))
                .replace("{{LAYOUT_ID}}", layout["layout_id"])
                .replace("{{SEMANTIC_ROLE}}", layout["semantic_role"])
                .replace("{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}", _format_layout_budget(layout))
    )
    return reviewer_client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt, schema=_slide_schema())



def _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], entry: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """The outline's layout decision is final. ``fill_slide`` is prompted with exactly one
    layout, but nothing stops the model from answering with a different one -- and content
    validated against one layout's budget, rendered through another's placeholders, is
    exactly the corrupted-but-plausible deck this whole module exists to prevent.
    """
    got = slide_dict.get("layout_id")
    want = entry["layout_id"]
    if got != want:
        raise CliWorkerSchemaError(
            f"slide {entry.get('id')!r}: the outline assigned layout_id {want!r}, but the "
            f"filled slide used {got!r}. Content must be written for the layout it was "
            "assigned -- re-fill it for the correct layout."
        )


def _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> Slide:
    """Structural validation JSON Schema cannot express: wraps the slide in a throwaway
    one-slide deck and reuses ``Deck.from_dict`` (ragged table rows, malformed rich text)
    plus ``validate_against_manifest`` (unknown ``layout_id`` -- belt-and-suspenders with
    ``_check_layout_matches_entry``, which catches the same defect earlier and by name).
    """
    throwaway = {
        "ir_version": IR_VERSION,
        "deck": {"title": slide_dict.get("title") or "untitled"},
        "slides": [slide_dict],
    }
    deck = Deck.from_dict(throwaway)
    errors = deck.validate_against_manifest(manifest)
    if errors:
        raise IRValidationError("slides[0].layout_id", "; ".join(errors))
    return deck.slides[0]


def _line_count(text: str, max_chars_per_line: int) -> int:
    """How many line-slots ``text`` costs against a placeholder's per-line character cap --
    the manifest's own rule ("a bullet that wraps consumes another slot"), applied
    literally rather than approximated by a raw character-count sum.
    """
    if not text:
        return 0
    return max(1, math.ceil(len(text) / max_chars_per_line))


def _block_text_items(block: Any) -> list[str]:
    """The text units a line-budget check measures one at a time. Each item consumes at
    least one line slot; a long item consumes ``ceil(len(item) / max_chars_per_line)``.

    ``code``, ``image`` and ``diagram`` blocks are deliberately excluded (empty list):
    they are not budgeted by character count (monospace/fixed-width, area, and Mermaid
    source respectively) -- that is real ``render/fit.py`` geometry work, still
    ``NotImplementedError`` there, and not something this bullet-shaped estimate should
    pretend to check.
    """
    if isinstance(block, Bullets):
        return [item.text.plain for item in block.items]
    if isinstance(block, Steps):
        return [f"{item.title} {item.text.plain if item.text else ''}".strip()
                for item in block.items]
    if isinstance(block, Cards):
        return [f"{item.title} {item.text.plain if item.text else ''}".strip()
                for item in block.items]
    if isinstance(block, Table):
        return [cell.plain for row in block.rows for cell in row]
    if isinstance(block, Paragraph):
        return [block.text.plain]
    if isinstance(block, Callout):
        return [f"{block.title or ''} {block.text.plain}".strip()]
    if isinstance(block, Heading):
        return [block.text]
    return []


def _check_slide_budget(slide: Slide, layout: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Local re-implementation of the check ``render/fit.py:check_budget`` still cannot do
    (it is ``NotImplementedError`` -- see that module's docstring). Deliberately simpler
    than the real thing: no word-wrap simulation, no per-character CJK-vs-Latin walk. It
    exists so the repair loop has something to catch an overflowing slide with, not to
    replace ``fit.py`` once it is implemented.

    Title-placeholder fallback, specific to THIS template: ``analyze_pptx_master.
    content_budget()`` only tags a placeholder's budget with ``max_title_chars_cjk`` when
    its ``ph_type`` is TITLE / CENTER_TITLE / VERTICAL_TITLE. Every placeholder in
    ``reference/layout_manifest.json`` is reported as plain ``BODY`` (confirmed directly
    against the manifest), so that tag never appears here and a title-vs-body split has to
    come from somewhere else. Placeholders are listed idx-ascending in the manifest, and
    idx-ascending is also top-to-bottom in every layout in this template (checked against
    each placeholder's ``inches.top``) -- so the first placeholder is used as the title's
    target, budgeted against its own single-line character cap. A template whose analyzer
    output DOES carry a real title tag is still handled correctly above it.

    Both title and body checks budget against the ``_cjk`` figures unconditionally, never
    ``_latin``: ``ir/schema.json``'s own ``deck.language`` doc says a ``"mixed"`` deck (the
    default) budgets against CJK as the pessimistic case, and this pipeline has no
    per-slide language signal to budget against ``_latin`` selectively even if it wanted
    to. Budgeting English content against the Korean cap is stricter than necessary, never
    unsafe -- the wrong direction to be wrong in.
    """
    placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
    if not placeholders:
        return  # blank/decoration-only layout -- nothing to check content against

    title_ph, *body_placeholders = placeholders
    title_budget = title_ph.get("content_budget") or {}
    if title_budget.get("computable"):
        title_cap = title_budget.get("max_title_chars_cjk",
                                     title_budget.get("max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"))
        if title_cap and len(slide.title) > title_cap:
            raise IRValidationError(
                "title",
                f"{len(slide.title)} chars exceeds this layout's {title_cap}-char title "
                f"budget (placeholder idx {title_ph['idx']}) -- cut it or split the slide"
            )

    for i, block in enumerate(slide.blocks):
        if i >= len(body_placeholders):
            break  # schema: overflow blocks fall through to free-floating shapes, unbudgeted
        budget = body_placeholders[i].get("content_budget") or {}
        if not budget.get("computable") or "max_bullets" not in budget:
            continue
        max_bullets = budget["max_bullets"]
        max_chars = budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"]
        total_lines = sum(_line_count(t, max_chars) for t in _block_text_items(block))
        if total_lines > max_bullets:
            raise IRValidationError(
                f"blocks[{i}]",
                f"needs {total_lines} lines but placeholder idx "
                f"{body_placeholders[i]['idx']} holds only {max_bullets} "
                f"(max {max_chars} korean chars per line) -- shorten it or split the slide"
            )


def generate_deck(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
                  manifest_path: Path, *, reviewer_client: LLMClient | None = None,
                  max_repairs: int = 2) -> GenerationResult:
    """Full two-stage generation with a bounded repair loop and optional reviewer pass.
    """
    manifest = json.loads(Path(manifest_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    outline = generate_outline(client, topic, sources, manifest)

    slides: list[Slide] = []
    total_attempts = 0
    any_repaired = False

    for entry in outline:
        layout = _find_layout(manifest, entry["layout_id"])
        repair_note = ""
        last_error: Exception | None = None
        slide: Slide | None = None
        attempt = 0

        for attempt in range(max_repairs + 1):
            total_attempts += 1
            try:
                slide_dict = fill_slide(client, entry, manifest, sources,
                                        repair_note=repair_note)
                if reviewer_client is not None:
                    try:
                        reviewed_dict = review_slide(reviewer_client, slide_dict, manifest)
                        slide_dict = reviewed_dict
                    except Exception as rev_exc:
                        # Log or fallback to fill_slide output if review fails
                        pass

                _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict, entry)
                parsed = _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict, manifest)
                _check_slide_budget(parsed, layout)
                slide = parsed
                break
            except (CliWorkerSchemaError, IRValidationError) as exc:
                last_error = exc
                repair_note = str(exc)

        if slide is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"slide {entry.get('id')!r} (layout {entry['layout_id']!r}) failed after "
                f"{max_repairs + 1} attempts: {last_error}"
            )
        if attempt > 0:
            any_repaired = True
        slides.append(slide)

    deck = Deck(deck=DeckMeta(title=topic[:120]), slides=slides)
    return GenerationResult(deck=deck, attempts=total_attempts, repaired=any_repaired,
                            token_usage={})


# [REDACTED]
# Headless CLI worker client -- no API, no HTTP
# [REDACTED]
#
# Reuses the locally-authenticated `claude`/`codex` CLIs instead of an API client.
# Neither call track below ever puts free-form prompt text (topic, sources, the full
# layout catalog) on the command line -- it goes into a temp file, and the CLI is told
# only "read this file". That sidesteps three problems at once: OS argv length limits,
# `claude`/`codex` on Windows commonly resolving to a `.cmd` shim (which cmd.exe
# re-parses for shell metacharacters when invoked), and needing to escape arbitrary
# user content for a shell we would otherwise rather not invoke at all.


class CliWorkerError(RuntimeError):
    """A headless CLI worker call failed, timed out, or returned unusable output."""


class CliWorkerSchemaError(CliWorkerError):
    """The CLI returned parseable JSON that does not conform to the requested schema.

    Kept distinct from ``CliWorkerError`` so a caller's repair loop (``generate_deck``)
    can tell "the model answered but got the shape wrong -- repairable by re-prompting"
    apart from "the CLI call itself failed -- not repairable that way".
    """


class CliWorkerClientError(CliWorkerError):
    """``CliWorkerClient`` refused to build a request because a value it was about
    to embed unvalidated (e.g. into generated YAML front matter) was unsafe.
    """


def _find_dotenv(filename: str = ".env") -> Path | None:
    for base in (Path(__file__).resolve().parent, Path.cwd()):
        for parent in (base, *base.parents):
            candidate = parent / filename
            if candidate.is_file():
                return candidate
    return None


_dotenv_loaded = False


def _load_dotenv_once() -> None:
    """Populate ``os.environ`` from ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env``, without clobbering
    variables the caller already set explicitly. Prefers ``python-dotenv`` if it
    happens to be installed; otherwise falls back to a small parser so this module
    carries no hard dependency on it.
    """
    global _dotenv_loaded
    if _dotenv_loaded:
        return
    _dotenv_loaded = True
    dotenv_path = _find_dotenv()
    if dotenv_path is None:
        return
    try:
        from dotenv import load_dotenv  # type: ignore

        load_dotenv(dotenv_path, override=False)
        return
    except ImportError:
        pass
    for raw_line in dotenv_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        line = raw_line.strip()
        if not line or line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
            continue
        key, _, value = line.partition("=")
        key = key.strip()
        value = value.strip()
        if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in "\"'":
            value = value[1:-1]
        os.environ.setdefault(key, value)


def _multiagent_root() -> str | None:
    """``MULTIAGENT_ROOT``, from the environment or ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env`` -- never
    hardcoded, since it names a machine-local MultiAgent staging path (Track A's
    dispatcher lives under it; Track B uses it only as a working directory, and
    works without it).
    """
    _load_dotenv_once()
    return os.environ.get("MULTIAGENT_ROOT") or None


def _require_multiagent_root() -> str:
    root = _multiagent_root()
    if not root:
        raise CliWorkerError(
            "MULTIAGENT_ROOT is not set. Add it to tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/.env, e.g.\n"
            '    MULTIAGENT_ROOT="/c/Users/<you>/.ma-stg/stg.<id>"\n'
            "or export it before constructing CliWorkerClient(track='a')."
        )
    return root


def _resolve_executable(name: str) -> str:
    resolved = shutil.which(name)
    if resolved is None:
        raise CliWorkerError(
            f"{name!r} not found on PATH -- install/log in to the CLI first; "
            "headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow."
        )
    return resolved


def _run_cli(cmd: list[str], *, cwd: str | None, timeout_s: int, input_str: str | None = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
    """``subprocess.run`` with controlled stdin/stdout and, on Windows
    only, ``shell=True`` so a ``.cmd``/``.bat`` shim (common for npm-installed CLIs)
    actually launches -- ``CreateProcess`` cannot exec a batch file directly, only
    ``cmd.exe`` can. Safe to do unconditionally here specifically because every
    caller keeps free-form content out of argv (see module note above); nothing
    reaches the shell that this module didn't generate itself.

    ``stderr`` is handed to the child as an open file (``_LIVE_LOG_PATH``), not
    ``subprocess.PIPE`` -- the OS then writes the worker's stderr straight to disk as the
    process produces it, so ``GET /api/log`` can tail a call that is still running instead
    of only ever seeing output after the call returns. The file is read back into the
    returned ``CompletedProcess.stderr`` before this function returns, so every existing
    caller that inspects ``result.stderr`` (e.g. Track B's non-zero-exit error message)
    keeps working exactly as before.
    """
    env = {**os.environ, "CI": "1"}
    use_shell = os.name == "nt" and cmd[0].lower().endswith((".cmd", ".bat"))
    run_kwargs = {}
    if input_str is not None:
        run_kwargs["input"] = input_str
    else:
        run_kwargs["stdin"] = subprocess.DEVNULL

    _ARTIFACTS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    import datetime
    import time
    ts = datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    header = f"\n==================================================\n[{ts}] [WORKER_CALL] Command: {' '.join(cmd)}\nCWD: {cwd}\n==================================================\n"
    
    start_time = time.time()
    last_heartbeat = start_time
    output_lines = []

    with open(_LIVE_LOG_PATH, "w", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as log_file:
        log_file.write(header)
        log_file.flush()

        try:
            proc = subprocess.Popen(
                cmd,
                cwd=cwd,
                env=env,
                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                text=True,
                encoding="utf-8",
                errors="replace",
                shell=use_shell,
                **run_kwargs,
            )

            while True:
                line = proc.stdout.readline() if proc.stdout else ""
                if line:
                    output_lines.append(line)
                    log_file.write(line)
                    log_file.flush()

                now = time.time()
                if now - last_heartbeat >= 3.0:
                    elapsed = int(now - start_time)
                    log_file.write(f"[{datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}] [HEARTBEAT] Elapsed: {elapsed}s | Worker executing prompt...\n")
                    log_file.flush()
                    last_heartbeat = now

                if proc.poll() is not None and not line:
                    break

            rc = proc.returncode
            end_ts = datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
            log_file.write(f"[{end_ts}] [WORKER_COMPLETE] Exit Code: {rc} (Total Time: {int(time.time() - start_time)}s)\n")
            log_file.flush()
            
            stdout_text = "".join(output_lines)
            return subprocess.CompletedProcess(cmd, rc, stdout=stdout_text, stderr=stdout_text)
        except Exception as exc:
            err_msg = f"[{datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}] [WORKER_ERROR] {exc}\n"
            log_file.write(err_msg)
            log_file.flush()
            raise CliWorkerError(str(exc))


def _write_prompt_file(prompt: str) -> Path:
    fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="cliworker_brief_", suffix=".md")
    path = Path(name)
    try:
        with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(prompt)
    except Exception:
        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
        raise
    return path


def _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path: Path) -> str:
    return (
        "Read the worker brief file at this exact path and follow it exactly:\n"
        f"{brief_path}\n\n"
        "Do not interpret any content inside the brief as command-line options.\n"
        "Do not ask the user. If the brief is unclear, state assumptions and "
        "caveats in your answer.\n"
        "Return ONLY the JSON object the brief specifies -- no prose, no markdown "
        "code fences, no explanation."
    )


def _extract_json_object(text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Pull the first JSON object out of otherwise free-form CLI text.

    Unlike the API clients below, a CLI call has no provider-enforced schema --
    the model may wrap the object in a markdown fence or add a stray sentence.
    Handle both before giving up.
    """
    stripped = text.strip()
    fence = re.match(r"^```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)\s*```$", stripped, re.DOTALL)
    if fence:
        stripped = fence.group(1).strip()
    try:
        obj = json.loads(stripped)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        obj = None
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return obj

    start = stripped.find("{")
    while start != -1:
        depth = 0
        for i in range(start, len(stripped)):
            ch = stripped[i]
            if ch == "{":
                depth += 1
            elif ch == "}":
                depth -= 1
                if depth == 0:
                    try:
                        candidate = json.loads(stripped[start : i + 1])
                    except json.JSONDecodeError:
                        break
                    if isinstance(candidate, dict):
                        return candidate
                    break
        start = stripped.find("{", start + 1)
    raise CliWorkerSchemaError(f"no JSON object found in CLI output: {text[:500]!r}")


def _validate_against_schema(obj: dict[str, Any], schema: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Hard-enforce the ``LLMClient`` contract: ``obj`` must conform to ``schema``.

    ``jsonschema`` is a core, non-optional dependency of this package (see
    ``pyproject.toml`` -- ``canvas/validate.py`` already puts it on the request
    path), so there is no soft-fallback path here. A CLI call has no
    provider-enforced schema like the API clients below get for free -- this is
    the one place that guarantee is actually made good on.

    ``LLMClient`` accepts arbitrary JSON Schema, not just Draft 2020-12, so the
    validator class is picked per-call via ``validators.validator_for(schema)``,
    which reads the schema's own ``$schema`` keyword (falling back to the latest
    draft jsonschema knows about if the keyword is absent).
    """
    validator_cls = validators.validator_for(schema)
    try:
        validator_cls(schema).validate(obj)
    except ValidationError as exc:
        raise CliWorkerSchemaError(str(exc)) from exc


class CliWorkerClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over a locally-authenticated ``claude``/``codex`` CLI -- no API
    key, no HTTP, no per-token billing beyond the CLI's own subscription. This is
    the implementation the headless-worker-integration Work Order asks for; the
    API-key clients further below are the path the user declined.

    Two call tracks, chosen with ``track=``:

    ``"b"`` (default) -- thin direct-CLI wrapper. Calls ``claude -p`` / ``codex
    exec`` straight, no dispatcher. Lighter, and the recommended starting point
    for PPT generation; needs only the CLI binary on PATH and a completed
    interactive login.

    ``"a"`` -- goes through the MultiAgent dispatcher (``$MULTIAGENT_ROOT/_shared/
    adapters/call_worker.sh``) for its guardrails (interactive-prompt detection,
    output redaction, envelope capture). Needs ``MULTIAGENT_ROOT`` set (see the
    module-level ``.env`` loader above) and a bash + coreutils environment.
    """

    _ALLOWED_MODELS = ("sonnet", "opus")

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        track: str = "b",
        backend: str = "claude",
        model: str = "sonnet",
        worker_role: str | None = None,
        timeout_s: int = 300,
    ) -> None:
        if track not in ("a", "b"):
            raise ValueError(f"track must be 'a' or 'b', got {track!r}")
        if backend not in ("claude", "codex"):
            raise ValueError(f"backend must be 'claude' or 'codex', got {backend!r}")
        if backend == "claude" and model not in self._ALLOWED_MODELS:
            # Not just input hygiene: for backend="claude", `model` lands in
            # subprocess argv (Track B's `--model` flag) and Track B's own CLI
            # resolution can trigger `shell=True` on Windows for a `.cmd`/`.bat`
            # shim (see `_run_cli`). Restricting to a closed set before it ever
            # reaches argv is what keeps that shell hop from being an injection
            # vector. Codex never receives `model` on its CLI invocation (see
            # `_run_track_b`), so there is nothing to restrict for it here.
            raise ValueError(f"model must be one of {self._ALLOWED_MODELS}, got {model!r}")
        self.track = track
        self.backend = backend
        self.model = model
        self.timeout_s = timeout_s
        if worker_role is not None:
            self.worker_role = worker_role
        elif backend == "codex":
            self.worker_role = "codex-critic"
        else:
            self.worker_role = "claude-review" if model == "opus" else "claude-review-sonnet"

    def complete_json(
        self,
        *,
        system: str,
        user: str,
        schema: dict[str, Any],
        max_tokens: int = 8000,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        # Neither CLI exposes a token-cap flag; length is governed by the per-placeholder
        # budgets already baked into `system` (see build_system_prompt), not this knob.
        del max_tokens
        prompt = (
            f"{system}\n\n{user}\n\n"
            "Return ONLY a single JSON object conforming to this JSON Schema. "
            "No prose, no markdown code fences, no explanation.\n\n"
            f"{json.dumps(schema, indent=2)}"
        )
        text = self._run_track_a(prompt) if self.track == "a" else self._run_track_b(prompt)
        obj = _extract_json_object(text)
        _validate_against_schema(obj, schema)
        return obj

    # -- Track A: through the dispatcher [REDACTED]

    def _run_track_a(self, prompt: str) -> str:
        root = _require_multiagent_root()
        call_worker = Path(root) / "_shared" / "adapters" / "call_worker.sh"
        if not call_worker.is_file():
            raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh not found at {call_worker}")
        bash_bin = _resolve_executable("bash")

        if self.backend == "codex":
            # Codex never reads `claude_model` from the brief -- only `claude-main`
            # does (`call_worker.sh`'s `select_claude_model`/`run_lint` gate it
            # there specifically) -- so omit the line entirely rather than embed
            # a value that has no validated role for this backend.
            front_matter = "---\nread_refs: N/A\n---\n\n"
        else:
            # `self.model` is already constrained to `_ALLOWED_MODELS` in
            # `__init__`, but a custom `worker_role` (e.g. "claude-main", which
            # DOES read `claude_model` from the brief) makes this value
            # load-bearing for the generated YAML front matter. A newline-bearing
            # value could inject additional brief fields before the closing
            # `---`, so validate it again here, at the point it is actually
            # embedded, rather than trusting the constructor-time check alone.
            if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+", self.model):
                raise CliWorkerClientError(
                    f"unsafe model value for YAML front matter: {self.model!r}"
                )
            front_matter = f"---\nread_refs: N/A\nclaude_model: {self.model}\n---\n\n"

        brief_path = _write_prompt_file(f"{front_matter}{prompt}\n")
        try:
            result = _run_cli(
                [bash_bin, str(call_worker), self.worker_role, str(brief_path)],
                cwd=root,
                timeout_s=self.timeout_s,
            )
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
            raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh timed out after {self.timeout_s}s") from exc
        finally:
            brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)

        if not result.stdout.strip():
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"call_worker.sh produced no envelope (rc={result.returncode}); "
                f"stderr: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
            )
        try:
            envelope = json.loads(result.stdout)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"call_worker.sh stdout is not a JSON envelope: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
            ) from exc

        if envelope.get("status") != "ok":
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"call_worker.sh returned status={envelope.get('status')!r} "
                f"(failure_class={envelope.get('failure_class')!r}, "
                f"exit_code={envelope.get('exit_code')!r}): "
                f"{str(envelope.get('stdout', ''))[:2000]}"
            )

        stdout_path = envelope.get("stdout_path")
        if stdout_path and Path(stdout_path).is_file():
            return Path(stdout_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
        return str(envelope.get("stdout", ""))

    # -- Track B: thin direct-CLI wrapper [REDACTED]

    def _run_track_b(self, prompt: str) -> str:
        brief_path = _write_prompt_file(prompt)
        try:
            wrapper_prompt = _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path)
            cwd = _multiagent_root()
            input_str = None

            if self.backend == "claude":
                cmd = [
                    _resolve_executable("claude"),
                    "-p", wrapper_prompt,
                    "--model", self.model,
                    "--output-format", "json",
                    "--allowedTools", "Read,Grep,Glob,LS",
                ]
            else:
                # For Codex, avoid argv length limits by passing prompt via stdin
                cmd = [_resolve_executable("codex"), "exec", "-"]
                input_str = prompt

            try:
                result = _run_cli(cmd, cwd=cwd, timeout_s=self.timeout_s, input_str=input_str)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
                raise CliWorkerError(
                    f"{self.backend} CLI timed out after {self.timeout_s}s -- confirm it is "
                    "logged in; headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow"
                ) from exc
        finally:
            brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)

        if result.returncode != 0:
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"{self.backend} CLI exited {result.returncode}: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
            )

        if self.backend == "codex":
            return result.stdout

        try:
            wrapper = json.loads(result.stdout)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"claude --output-format json returned non-JSON: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
            ) from exc
        text = wrapper.get("result")
        if not isinstance(text, str):
            raise CliWorkerError(f"unexpected claude JSON wrapper shape: {wrapper!r}")
        return text


# [REDACTED]
# Reference client implementations
# [REDACTED]

class ClaudeClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over the Anthropic Messages API.

    Uses tool-use to force schema conformance::

        client.messages.create(
            model="claude-sonnet-5",           # or claude-opus-4-8 for the outline stage
            system=system,
            messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
            tools=[{"name": "emit_slide_ir",
                    "description": "Emit the slide IR.",
                    "input_schema": schema}],
            tool_choice={"type": "tool", "name": "emit_slide_ir"},   # forces the call
            max_tokens=max_tokens,
        )

    then read ``response.content[0].input`` -- already a dict, already schema-valid.

    Cost note: the system prompt (catalog + budgets) is identical across every slide in a
    deck, so mark it ``cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral"}`` and pay for it once.
    """

    def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
                      max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
        raise NotImplementedError


class OpenAIClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over OpenAI Structured Outputs.

    ``response_format={"type": "json_schema", "json_schema": {"name": "slide_ir",
    "schema": schema, "strict": True}}``.

    Caveat that will bite: ``strict: true`` rejects several constructs our schema uses --
    ``oneOf`` at the top of a ``$defs`` union, ``default``, ``format: uri``, and unbounded
    ``additionalProperties``. Keep a *derived* strict-mode variant of the schema rather
    than contorting the canonical one; the canonical schema serves Claude and Gemini
    unmodified and must stay readable.
    """

    def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
                      max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
        raise NotImplementedError


class GeminiClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over Gemini's ``response_schema``.

    Gemini accepts an OpenAPI-3 subset, not full JSON Schema: no ``$defs``/``$ref``,
    no ``oneOf`` in older model revisions. The IR schema must be *inlined and flattened*
    for this provider. Write that transform once, in a helper, and test it -- do not
    maintain a second hand-written copy of the schema, which will drift.
    """

    def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
                      max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
        raise NotImplementedError

===== tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\src\aippt\canvas\app.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""The same-origin editor server. Plan Sec 9, Phase 1C tasks 3.1-3.3, pulled forward into 1B.

    python -m aippt.canvas.app        # -> http://127.0.0.1:8000

THIS IS AN ADAPTER, NOT A SECOND RENDERER. It owns exactly two things the CLI does not: it serves
the editor's static files same-origin, and it hands a JSON body to render_canvas.render(). Every
rule it appears to enforce -- the schema, the reserved regions, the accent1 ban, the template pin --
is enforced by the frozen backend; this module only maps the resulting exception onto an HTTP status
a browser can act on. If this file and the CLI ever disagree about the same payload, the bug is
here.

THE CLI STAYS FIRST-CLASS (task 3.3). Corporate IT blocks localhost servers often enough that
`python -m aippt.canvas.render_canvas deck.json -o out.pptx` is the fallback that always works. So
the dependency arrow points one way only: app.py imports the canvas package, and NOTHING under
canvas/ imports app.py. FastAPI stays an OPTIONAL extra (`pip install -e ".[editor]"`) and the
renderer installs and runs without it.

THE SERVER WRITES NO FILES FOR RENDERING. prs.save() goes to a BytesIO. There is no temp directory,
no output path, and no filename taken from the payload -- `target.template` is informational and is
ignored (schema.json says so in as many words). Honouring a path from the body would be an
arbitrary-file read, which is precisely the hole task 3.2 exists to close.

AI_PPT_v2 PHASE 4 ADDS THE ONE EXCEPTION: /api/designs. A user-named canvas-ir/1 deck is written to
SAVED_DECKS_DIR, a single fixed directory next to this file -- never a path the request chooses. The
name is validated against a strict allowlist before it ever reaches Path() (_validate_design_name /
_design_path below), so this remains not an arbitrary-file write: the caller picks a filename stem
inside one sandboxed folder and nothing else about the path.

/api/generate HAS TWO MODES, AND THE ENVIRONMENT PICKS ONE (Phase 4; repointed at a headless CLI
worker by the CliWorkerClient migration). Without AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1 it is the Phase 3 MOCK: it
calls no model, reaches no network, and reads nothing from the prompt -- one hardcoded canvas-ir/1
deck, 1.5 s late. With AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1 it is the LIVE author: it reads the prompt, calls a
locally-authenticated claude/codex CLI through CliWorkerClient.complete_json()
(aippt.adapters.ai_adapter -- no API key, no HTTP), and assembles the reply into a deck. Either way
the editor's request shape, its loading state, its failure paths and its hand-off to loadIR() are
identical, and either way the deck goes through validate() before it answers -- a mock that ships an
invalid deck would prove the wiring works while quietly breaking the only thing the wiring carries,
and a model is exactly the author most likely to put a box on the logo. The mode is read at REQUEST
time (server_mode()), so the same process serves whichever the environment says.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import base64
import binascii
import io
import json
import logging
import os
import re
import unicodedata
import urllib.parse
import tempfile
import shutil
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

LIVE_LOG_PATH = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "artifacts" / "multiagent_live.log"
LIVE_LOG_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

from fastapi import FastAPI, Request, Response, UploadFile
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse, JSONResponse
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

from aippt.adapters.ai_adapter import CliWorkerClient, CliWorkerError
from aippt.canvas.geometry import CANVAS
from aippt.canvas.pin import TemplatePinError, load_pin, verify_pin
from aippt.canvas.render_canvas import render
from aippt.canvas.style import BRAND_RED, EA, FORBIDDEN, LATIN
from aippt.canvas.validate import CanvasValidationError, SCHEMA_PATH, StalePayloadError, validate

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

STATIC_DIR = Path(__file__).with_name("static")

PPTX_MIME = "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.presentationml.presentation"

# A one-slide deck of boxes and arrows is a few KB of JSON. Anything approaching a megabyte is a
# mistake or an attack, and either way the renderer should not be the thing that discovers it.
# Note: Increased to 50MB to support the Add Slide To feature (sending target PPTX as base64 in JSON).
MAX_BODY_BYTES = 50 * 1024 * 1024

DEFAULT_STEM = "deck"

# The latency an LLM call would cost, simulated. It is not padding: the editor's loading state is
# real UI with a real failure mode (a button you can press twice), and a mock that answered
# instantly would never exercise it. It is spent ONLY on the success path -- a malformed request is
# refused immediately, because no model would have been called for that one either.
MOCK_LATENCY_S = 1.5

# Everything a filesystem, a Content-Disposition header or a shell could misread. The title is
# author-controlled text and it NEVER reaches the filesystem -- but it does reach a header and then
# a browser's Save dialog, so it is sanitized on the way out regardless.
_UNSAFE_STEM = re.compile(r'[\x00-\x1f\x7f"\\/:*?<>|]+')

# Phase 4: the live author. The ENVIRONMENT, not the code, chooses the mode -- AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1
# means "call a real CLI worker"; absent (or anything else) means "serve the Phase 3 mock".

# --- the model allowlist (Phase 1; repointed at CliWorkerClient by the headless-worker migration) -
#
# THE CLIENT PICKS A PROFILE, NEVER A MODEL ID DIRECTLY. Everything the editor can ask for is a key
# of this dict, and the (backend, model) pair behind it -- the exact constructor arguments
# CliWorkerClient(backend=..., model=...) takes -- is chosen HERE, by the server. A body carrying
# "model": "gpt-9-ultra" (or "../../etc/passwd") is refused by membership before any subprocess ever
# runs, and re-pointing a profile at a different CLI model is a one-line server change that no
# browser has to hear about. `thinking` is published to the editor (see /api/context) as a UI hint
# only -- CliWorkerClient has no thinking-level knob of its own, unlike the google-genai client this
# replaced.
DEFAULT_PROFILE = "sonnet"

MODEL_PROFILES: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {
    "sonnet": {
        "label": "Claude Sonnet 5", "thinking": "MEDIUM", "backend": "claude", "model": "sonnet",
    },
    "opus": {
        "label": "Claude Opus", "thinking": "HIGH", "backend": "claude", "model": "opus",
    },
    "gpt-5.6-terra": {
        "label": "GPT 5.6 Terra", "thinking": "HIGH", "backend": "codex", "model": "gpt-5.6-terra",
    },
    "gpt-5.6-sol": {
        "label": "GPT 5.6 Sol", "thinking": "HIGH", "backend": "codex", "model": "gpt-5.6-sol",
    },
}

# --- the image attachment limits (Phase 1) [REDACTED]
#
# MAX_BODY_BYTES (2 MB) already caps the wire, but it caps the WRONG number: base64 expands 4:3, so
# the most that can arrive intact is ~1.5 MB decoded, and a caller who trips the body limit gets a
# 413 about a number they never chose. These caps are stated in the units the caller actually sent
# (a count of images, a total of decoded bytes) and are published in /api/context so the editor can
# refuse locally instead of discovering the limit by being refused.
#
# The count limit is NOT redundant with the byte limit: twenty 60 KB thumbnails fit under the byte
# ceiling comfortably and would still make one very slow, very expensive call.
MAX_BODY_BYTES = 100 * 1024 * 1024  # 100MB for PPTX/media attachments
MAX_IMAGES = 4
MAX_IMAGE_BYTES = 1_400_000

# The MIME types this server accepts and validates. Historically fed straight into the Gemini
# vision path; CliWorkerClient (the current live author -- see generate_live()) has no attachment
# parameter at all, so an accepted image is validated and then DISCARDED, never forwarded to the
# model. Kept EXACT after normalisation regardless -- never a `startswith("image/")` test, which
# would wave through image/svg+xml (an XML document with script in it) as an "image".
IMAGE_MIME_TYPES = ("image/png", "image/jpeg", "image/webp", "image/heic", "image/heif")

# Two spellings of the same field, in precedence order. The editor's own upload code and a former
# Gemini-SDK-shaped payload disagreed about the name ("mime" vs "mime_type"); both are still
# accepted here for compatibility, and a 422 over a synonym would be a cross-phase break with no
# user-visible cause. Both are read; `mime` is canonical downstream.
_MIME_KEYS = ("mime", "mime_type")

# The system instruction the live author injects. It is the SAME contract the Web-Gem workflow
# pastes into gemini.google.com, kept in one file so the two paths cannot drift. It lives at the
# project root: canvas/ -> aippt/ -> src/ -> <project root>.
PROMPT_PATH = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "GEM_Knowledge_API.md"

# Multi-agent live log (Phase 2/3): ai_adapter.py's CliWorkerClient mirrors the worker subprocess's
# stderr to this exact path in real time (see ai_adapter._run_cli) -- same "canvas/ -> aippt/ ->
# src/ -> project root" convention as PROMPT_PATH above, so both modules agree on the path without
# either importing the other's constant.
LIVE_LOG_PATH = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "artifacts" / "multiagent_live.log"


# --- server-side design storage (AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4) [REDACTED]
#
# THE ONE EXCEPTION TO "THE SERVER WRITES NO FILES FOR RENDERING", see the module docstring above.
# SAVED_DECKS_DIR is a single fixed directory next to this file -- never a path the request chooses
# -- and every name that reaches it is validated by _validate_design_name()/_design_path() below
# before Path() ever sees it, so this stays a sandboxed, allowlisted write, not an arbitrary one.
SAVED_DECKS_DIR = Path(__file__).with_name("saved_decks")
SAVED_DECKS_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)

# The same forbidden-character set _UNSAFE_STEM strips out of a downloaded filename, but as an
# allowlist anchored end to end (^...$) rather than a strip-and-continue substitution: a design name
# that FAILS this match is refused outright with a 422, not silently mangled into something the
# caller never typed.
_SAFE_DESIGN_NAME = re.compile(r'^[^\x00-\x1f\x7f\\/:*?"<>|]{1,80}$')

# Windows reserves these device names AT THE FILESYSTEM LEVEL, regardless of extension: "CON.json"
# opens the console device exactly like "CON" does, so a save/load through SAVED_DECKS_DIR would
# either fail outright or silently miss the file entirely. Checked case-insensitively against the
# portion of the name before its FIRST '.' -- the same portion that ends up before the first '.' in
# the on-disk "<name>.json", which is what Windows actually looks at (a raw suffix like "my.con"
# is fine; the reserved word has to be the leading component, not a trailing one).
_RESERVED_DEVICE_NAMES = frozenset(
    {"CON", "PRN", "AUX", "NUL"}
    | {f"COM{d}" for d in "123456789"}
    | {f"LPT{d}" for d in "123456789"}
)


class LiveGenerateError(RuntimeError):
    """A live model call failed -- the mock path (AIPPT_LIVE_MODE unset) NEVER raises this.

    Carries a human reason the editor shows verbatim. Deliberately NOT a CanvasValidationError: a
    model that returns unusable text is an upstream (502) failure, not a user's invalid deck (422).
    """


@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
    """Both pin gates, ONCE, at process start -- never per request.

    TemplatePinError is deliberately NOT caught: an unverified template must stop the process from
    starting at all. A server that boots and then 500s on every export has told the operator the
    same thing far less clearly, and a server that quietly falls back to another layout has told
    them nothing at all and will ship a wrong-looking deck (pin.py's whole reason to exist).
    """
    pin = load_pin()
    verify_pin(pin)
    app.state.pin = pin
    log.info(
        "pin OK: %s @ %s",
        pin["renderer_template"]["filename"],
        pin["renderer_template"]["sha256"],
    )
    yield


app = FastAPI(
    title="aippt canvas editor",
    version="1",
    lifespan=lifespan,
    # No CORS middleware, on purpose. The editor is served from this same origin; a browser needs
    # no cross-origin grant to talk to it, and adding one would only widen who can.
)

app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=STATIC_DIR), name="static")


@app.get("/")
async def index() -> FileResponse:
    return FileResponse(STATIC_DIR / "index.html", media_type="text/html; charset=utf-8")


@app.get("/api/context")
async def api_context(request: Request) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Everything the editor must NOT hard-code.

    Chiefly the template SHA. If the editor carried a literal SHA, re-pinning the template -- a
    deliberate, reviewed act (Sec 4.3) -- would turn into a silent 409 for every user of every
    stale browser tab, and the fix would be "hard-refresh", which nobody would guess. Serving it
    keeps reference/template_pin.json the single source of truth on both sides of the wire.

    THE SAME ARGUMENT NOW COVERS THE MODEL PICKER AND THE ATTACHMENT LIMITS (Phase 1). A picker with
    hardcoded options drifts from the server's allowlist the day a profile is added or renamed, and
    every drift is a 422 the user cannot read. `models` / `default_model` / `images` are the server
    telling the editor what it will accept, so both sides cannot disagree.

    EVERY PRE-EXISTING KEY IS UNCHANGED IN SHAPE. `model` still reports a human string in live mode
    (the badge tooltip) -- it is not the profile id. It used to report the single Gemini deployment
    model every profile shared; now every profile is a genuinely different (backend, model) pair
    (see MODEL_PROFILES), so there is no longer one provider model to report globally. It reports
    the DEFAULT profile's label instead -- a caller who named a different profile on the request
    itself already knows which one it asked for.
    """
    pin = request.app.state.pin
    return {
        "template_sha256": pin["renderer_template"]["sha256"],
        "layout_index": pin["layout_signature"]["layout_index"],
        "canvas": dict(CANVAS),
        "brand": {"red": f"#{BRAND_RED}", "forbidden": f"#{FORBIDDEN}"},
        "fonts": {"latin": LATIN, "ea": EA},
        "mode": server_mode(),
        "model": MODEL_PROFILES[DEFAULT_PROFILE]["label"] if server_mode() == "live" else None,
        "models": [
            {"id": pid, "label": prof["label"], "thinking": prof["thinking"]}
            for pid, prof in MODEL_PROFILES.items()
        ],
        "default_model": DEFAULT_PROFILE,
        "images": {
            "max_count": MAX_IMAGES,
            "max_bytes": MAX_IMAGE_BYTES,
            "mime_types": list(IMAGE_MIME_TYPES),
            # Ordered: the first one present on an object wins. Published so a client does not have
            # to guess which spelling this server speaks -- it speaks both.
            "mime_keys": list(_MIME_KEYS),
        },
    }


@app.get("/api/log")
async def api_get_log() -> Response:
    """Returns multi-agent live execution logs."""
    if not LIVE_LOG_PATH.is_file():
        return JSONResponse({"log": "[INFO] No log file found yet. Waiting for worker executions..."})
    try:
        content = LIVE_LOG_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
        if not content.strip():
            content = "[INFO] Log file is currently empty. Waiting for worker executions..."
        return JSONResponse({"log": content})
    except Exception as exc:
        return JSONResponse({"error": str(exc)}, status_code=500)


@app.post("/api/download")
async def api_export(request: Request) -> Response:
    """canvas-ir/1 in, .pptx bytes out. In memory, start to finish.

    The error mapping is the whole job, and the ORDER of the except clauses is the load-bearing
    part: StalePayloadError SUBCLASSES CanvasValidationError, so catching the parent first would
    report every stale payload as a 422 and tell the user to fix a deck that is perfectly fine. It
    is caught first, and it stays first.
    """
    ir, err = await _read_json(request)
    if err is not None:
        return err

    try:
        prs = render(ir)  # validates, checks the pin, checks the SHA, draws
    except StalePayloadError as exc:  # MUST precede CanvasValidationError -- it is a subclass
        return JSONResponse({"error": "stale", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=409)
    except CanvasValidationError as exc:
        return JSONResponse({"error": "validation", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=422)
    except TemplatePinError as exc:
        # Unreachable if the startup gate ran -- the template cannot drift mid-process without
        # someone editing it underneath us. Log it loudly rather than pretend it cannot happen.
        log.exception("template pin failed at REQUEST time; the startup gate should have caught it")
        return JSONResponse({"error": "template", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)

    buf = io.BytesIO()
    prs.save(buf)  # in memory. No temp file, no output directory, nothing on disk.

    return Response(
        content=buf.getvalue(),
        media_type=PPTX_MIME,
        headers={"Content-Disposition": content_disposition(ir["title"])},
    )


@app.post("/api/append_slide")
async def api_append_slide(request: Request) -> Response:
    ir, media_map, err, tmp_dir = await _read_multipart_ir(request)
    if err is not None:
        if tmp_dir:
            import shutil
            shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir, ignore_errors=True)
        return err

    target_b64 = ir.get("target", {}).get("target_pptx_b64")
    if not target_b64:
        if tmp_dir:
            import shutil
            shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir, ignore_errors=True)
        return JSONResponse({"error": "missing_target", "detail": "Target PPTX base64 missing."}, status_code=400)
    
    del ir["target"]["target_pptx_b64"]
    target_bytes = base64.b64decode(target_b64)

    try:
        try:
            from aippt.canvas.render_canvas import render_append
            prs = render_append(ir, target_bytes, media_map=media_map)
        except StalePayloadError as exc:
            return JSONResponse({"error": "stale", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=409)
        except CanvasValidationError as exc:
            return JSONResponse({"error": "validation", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=422)
        except TemplatePinError as exc:
            log.exception("template pin failed at REQUEST time")
            return JSONResponse({"error": "template", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)
        except Exception as exc:
            log.exception("append slide failed")
            return JSONResponse({"error": "server_error", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)

        buf = io.BytesIO()
        prs.save(buf)
    finally:
        if tmp_dir:
            import shutil
            shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir, ignore_errors=True)

    return Response(
        content=buf.getvalue(),
        media_type=PPTX_MIME,
        headers={"Content-Disposition": content_disposition(ir.get("title", ""))},
    )


@app.post("/api/generate")
async def api_generate(request: Request) -> Response:
    """{"prompt", "model"?, "images"?} in, canvas-ir/1 out. MOCK without AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1, LIVE with it.

    Either way the editor hands what comes back straight to loadIR(), which judges every element
    before it touches the stage -- so a model draft is no more trusted than a hand-edited file. The
    request shape, the loading state and the hand-off are identical in both modes; only the author
    behind the seam changes.

    `prompt` IS STILL THE ONLY MANDATORY FIELD (Phase 1). `{"prompt": "..."}` on its own means what
    it has always meant: the default profile, no attachments. The two new fields are additive.

    EVERY CHECK RUNS BEFORE THE MODE BRANCH. A body naming a profile that does not exist, or one
    carrying an image that is not one, is refused identically on a laptop with AIPPT_LIVE_MODE unset
    and on one with it set -- so a client bug cannot hide behind mock mode until it reaches
    production. It also means those refusals cost neither the mock's 1.5 s nor a CLI round trip.
    """
    body, err = await _read_json(request)
    if err is not None:
        return err

    prompt = body.get("prompt") if isinstance(body, dict) else None
    if not isinstance(prompt, str) or not prompt.strip():
        # Refused BEFORE any model call or sleep. No author would have been invoked for an empty body.
        return JSONResponse(
            {"error": "validation", "detail": 'the body must be {"prompt": "<non-empty text>"}'},
            status_code=422,
        )

    profile, err = _resolve_profile(body.get("model"))
    if err is not None:
        return err

    do_review = bool(body.get("review")) if isinstance(body, dict) else False
    reviewer_model = body.get("reviewer_model") if isinstance(body, dict) else None

    images, err = _decode_images(body.get("images"))
    if err is not None:
        return err

    pin = request.app.state.pin

    # --- MOCK: AIPPT_LIVE_MODE is not set. The prompt is NOT read; a fixed deck comes back 1.5 s late.
    if not live_mode_enabled():
        # Logged BY LENGTH, never by content: this is an unauthenticated local endpoint and the deck
        # text is the user's, not ours.
        #
        # THE PROFILE AND THE IMAGES WERE VALIDATED AND ARE NOW DELIBERATELY DROPPED. A mock that
        # varied with either would stop being a mock: [REDACTED]
        # requires two different requests to produce byte-identical decks, and that property is the
        # only reason the mock is worth having.
        log.info(
            "mock generate: %d-char prompt, %d image(s), profile %s (none of it is read -- "
            "no model is called)",
            len(prompt), len(images), profile,
        )
        ir = mock_ir(pin)

        # THE MOCK IS VALIDATED BEFORE IT IS SERVED, by the same validator the export path runs. It
        # cannot fail because of anything the caller sent -- so if it fails, it is OUR bug: a 500.
        try:
            validate(ir)
        except CanvasValidationError as exc:
            log.exception("the MOCK payload does not validate -- a server bug, not a user error")
            return JSONResponse({"error": "mock", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)

        await asyncio.sleep(MOCK_LATENCY_S)
        return JSONResponse(ir)

    # --- LIVE: a real CLI-worker call. The prompt IS read now. [REDACTED]
    cfg = MODEL_PROFILES[profile]
    log.info(
        "live generate: %d-char prompt, %d image(s) (validated, not forwarded -- see "
        "generate_live()), profile %s (%s thinking), review=%s (reviewer=%s) -> backend=%s model=%s",
        len(prompt), len(images), profile, cfg["thinking"], do_review, reviewer_model, cfg["backend"], cfg["model"],
    )
    try:
        ir = await generate_live(
            prompt.strip(), pin, profile=profile, images=images,
            review=do_review, reviewer_model=reviewer_model
        )
    except LiveGenerateError as exc:
        log.warning("live generate failed: %s", exc)
        return JSONResponse({"error": "generate", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=502)

    # The assembled deck goes through the SAME validator the export path runs. A model can put a box
    # on the logo as easily as a human can; refusing it here means the editor is never handed a draft
    # it could only bounce, and the detail is the same numbered list validate() gives everywhere.
    try:
        validate(ir)
    except CanvasValidationError as exc:
        return JSONResponse({"error": "model", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=502)

    return JSONResponse(ir)


@app.get("/api/designs")
async def api_list_designs() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Every design saved so far, by name. AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4.

    Sorted so the editor's dropdown does not reorder itself between one load and the next just
    because the filesystem happened to iterate `saved_decks/` in a different order this time.
    """
    names = sorted(p.stem for p in SAVED_DECKS_DIR.glob("*.json") if p.is_file())
    return {"designs": names}


@app.post("/api/designs")
async def api_save_design(request: Request) -> Response:
    """{"name", "ir"} in, the design written to disk. AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4.

    THE ONE ROUTE THAT WRITES A FILE, and it is deliberately narrow: `ir` goes through the SAME
    validate() /api/export runs, so a saved design can never be a deck this server would later
    refuse to load back -- and `name` goes through _validate_design_name()/_design_path(), so the
    write can only ever land inside SAVED_DECKS_DIR under a name matching the allowlist.
    """
    body, err = await _read_json(request)
    if err is not None:
        return err
    if not isinstance(body, dict):
        return _rejected(
            "body_shape", 'the body must be {"name": "<string>", "ir": <canvas-ir/1 object>}'
        )

    name, err = _validate_design_name(body.get("name"))
    if err is not None:
        return err

    ir = body.get("ir")
    if not isinstance(ir, dict):
        return _rejected("body_shape", "'ir' must be a canvas-ir/1 object")
    try:
        validate(ir)
    except CanvasValidationError as exc:
        return JSONResponse({"error": "validation", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=422)

    try:
        path = _design_path(name)
    except ValueError as exc:
        log.exception("a design name passed validation but _design_path() refused it anyway")
        return JSONResponse({"error": "name", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)

    path.write_text(json.dumps(ir, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    return JSONResponse({"name": name})


@app.get("/api/designs/{name}")
async def api_load_design(name: str) -> Response:
    """A saved design, by name. AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4. 404 if it was never saved, or was deleted."""
    name, err = _validate_design_name(name)
    if err is not None:
        return err
    try:
        path = _design_path(name)
    except ValueError as exc:
        log.exception("a design name passed validation but _design_path() refused it anyway")
        return JSONResponse({"error": "name", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)
    if not path.is_file():
        return JSONResponse(
            {"error": "not_found", "detail": f"no saved design named {name!r}"}, status_code=404
        )
    try:
        data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
        log.exception("a saved design on disk could not be read back")
        return JSONResponse({"error": "corrupt", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)
    return JSONResponse(data)


@app.delete("/api/designs/{name}")
async def api_delete_design(name: str) -> Response:
    """Deletes a saved design by name."""
    name, err = _validate_design_name(name)
    if err is not None:
        return err
    try:
        path = _design_path(name)
    except ValueError as exc:
        log.exception("a design name passed validation but _design_path() refused it anyway")
        return JSONResponse({"error": "name", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)
    if not path.is_file():
        return JSONResponse(
            {"error": "not_found", "detail": f"no saved design named {name!r}"}, status_code=404
        )
    try:
        path.unlink()
    except OSError as exc:
        log.exception("could not delete saved design")
        return JSONResponse({"error": "delete", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)
    return Response(status_code=204)



async def _read_multipart_ir(request: Request) -> tuple[Any, dict[str, str] | None, JSONResponse | None, str | None]:
    declared = request.headers.get("content-length")
    if declared is not None and declared.isdigit() and int(declared) > MAX_BODY_BYTES:
        return None, None, _too_large(), None

    try:
        form = await request.form(max_part_size=100 * 1024 * 1024)
    except Exception as exc:
        return None, None, JSONResponse({"error": "malformed", "detail": f"body is not valid multipart/form-data: {exc}"}, status_code=400), None

    ir_field = form.get("ir")
    if ir_field is None:
        return None, None, JSONResponse({"error": "validation", "detail": 'the form must include a field named "ir"'}, status_code=422), None
    
    ir_raw = await ir_field.read() if isinstance(ir_field, UploadFile) else ir_field.encode("utf-8")

    try:
        ir = json.loads(ir_raw.decode("utf-8"))
    except (UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
        return None, None, JSONResponse({"error": "malformed", "detail": f'the "ir" field is not valid UTF-8 JSON: {exc}'}, status_code=400), None

    tmp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="aippt-media-")
    media_map: dict[str, str] = {}
    for name, value in form.multi_items():
        if name == "ir" or not isinstance(value, UploadFile):
            continue
        filename = Path(value.filename or name).name
        dest = Path(tmp_dir) / filename
        dest.write_bytes(await value.read())
        media_map[filename] = str(dest)
        
    return ir, media_map, None, tmp_dir


async def _read_json(request: Request) -> tuple[Any, JSONResponse | None]:
    """The body guard every POST route shares. Returns (payload, None) or (None, error).

    Both size checks are needed and both are here: a chunked body declares no Content-Length, so the
    header check alone can be walked straight past, and the real-length check alone would have us
    buffer a megabyte before objecting to it.
    """
    declared = request.headers.get("content-length")
    if declared is not None and declared.isdigit() and int(declared) > MAX_BODY_BYTES:
        return None, _too_large()

    raw = await request.body()
    if len(raw) > MAX_BODY_BYTES:  # chunked bodies declare no length; check the real thing too
        return None, _too_large()

    try:
        return json.loads(raw.decode("utf-8")), None
    except (UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
        # str(exc) is json's own "Expecting ',' delimiter: line 3 column 9" -- useful, and it leaks
        # nothing but the shape of the body the caller just sent us.
        return None, JSONResponse(
            {"error": "malformed", "detail": f"body is not valid UTF-8 JSON: {exc}"},
            status_code=400,
        )


def _too_large() -> JSONResponse:
    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "error": "too_large",
            "detail": f"payload exceeds {MAX_BODY_BYTES} bytes",
        },
        status_code=413,
    )


# --- the request validators (Phase 1) [REDACTED]
#
# Both return (value, None) or (None, error) -- the same two-tuple shape _read_json() already uses,
# so api_generate() reads as one flat sequence of guards rather than a nest of try blocks. Both run
# BEFORE the mode branch: what the server accepts must not depend on whether a key happens to be set
# on this machine.


def _rejected(code: str, detail: str) -> JSONResponse:
    """A 422 with a machine-readable discriminator.

    `"error": "validation"` is deliberately UNCHANGED -- the editor's existing banner keys on it
    and would go blank if it were renamed. `"code"` is additive, for a client that wants to say
    something more specific than the sentence we wrote.
    """
    return JSONResponse({"error": "validation", "code": code, "detail": detail}, status_code=422)


def _resolve_profile(raw: Any) -> tuple[str | None, JSONResponse | None]:
    """The requested profile, or a 422. Absent means the default -- a legacy body is fine."""
    if raw is None:
        return DEFAULT_PROFILE, None
    if not isinstance(raw, str) or raw not in MODEL_PROFILES:
        return None, _rejected(
            "unknown_model",
            f"{raw!r} is not an available model; choose one of {sorted(MODEL_PROFILES)}",
        )
