  851: @lru_cache(maxsize=1)
  852: def _relaxed_live_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
  853:     """canvas/schema.json (the SAME file validate.py enforces at export time) with `target` dropped
  854:     from the top-level `required` list -- everything else, including `target`'s own inner schema for
  855:     when a model DOES include it, stays exactly as strict.
  856: 
  857:     The model cannot know reference/template_pin.json's current SHA or the pinned layout index --
  858:     both are injected from the LIVE pin by _finalize_live_ir() after the call returns, never
  859:     authored by the model. Relaxing anything beyond this one field would let a model skip work
  860:     _finalize_live_ir() does not do on its behalf.
  861:     """
  862:     schema = json.loads(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
  863:     schema["required"] = [name for name in schema["required"] if name != "target"]
  864:     return schema
  865: 
  866: 
  867: def _finalize_live_ir(data: Any, pin: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
  868:     """Overwrite schema, canvas and target from the LIVE PIN -- the model is never trusted with them.
  869: 
  870:     The system prompt tells the model to omit target entirely (it has no business choosing our
  871:     template SHA) and to fix schema and canvas to constants. We ENFORCE all three here rather than
  872:     hope: a model that ignores the instruction does not get to author the pin. Everything else --
  873:     title and elements -- is the model's, and validate() judges it next.
  874:     """
  875:     if not isinstance(data, dict):
  876:         raise LiveGenerateError(f"the model returned a {type(data).__name__}, not a JSON object")
  877:     ir = dict(data)
  878:     ir["schema"] = "canvas-ir/1"
  879:     ir["canvas"] = dict(CANVAS)
  880:     ir["target"] = {
  881:         "template": pin["renderer_template"]["filename"],
  882:         "template_sha256": pin["renderer_template"]["sha256"],
  883:         "layout_index": pin["layout_signature"]["layout_index"],
  884:     }
  885:     ir.setdefault("title", "")
  886:     if not isinstance(ir.get("elements"), list):
  887:         ir["elements"] = []
  888: 
  889:     # Robustness fallback: Models often omit `color` on runs that are just whitespace (" ").
  890:     # The schema requires `color` on EVERY run. We inject a default to prevent failing the whole deck.
  891:     for el in ir["elements"]:
  892:         if isinstance(el, dict):
  893:             # Connector arrow fallback: The schema requires `arrow` on connectors.
  894:             # Models sometimes omit it for simple lines. Default to "none".
  895:             if el.get("type") == "connector" and "arrow" not in el:
  896:                 el["arrow"] = "none"
  897: 
  898:             if isinstance(el.get("text"), dict):
  899:                 for p in el["text"].get("paragraphs", []):
  900:                     if isinstance(p, dict) and isinstance(p.get("runs"), list):
  901:                         for run in p["runs"]:
  902:                             if isinstance(run, dict) and "color" not in run:
  903:                                 run["color"] = "#1A1A1A"
  904: 
  905:     return ir
  906: 
  907: 
  908: async def generate_live(
  909:     prompt: str,
  910:     pin: dict[str, Any],
  911:     profile: str = DEFAULT_PROFILE,
  912:     images: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
  913:     review: bool = False,
  914:     reviewer_model: str | None = None,
  915: ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  916:     """prompt (+ profile) -> canvas-ir/1 for the live author with optional review pass."""
  917:     del images  # accepted for signature/contract compatibility only -- see docstring above
  918: 
  919:     cfg = MODEL_PROFILES[profile]
  920:     client = CliWorkerClient(track="b", backend=cfg["backend"], model=cfg["model"], timeout_s=900)
  921: 
  922:     try:
  923:         obj = await asyncio.to_thread(
  924:             client.complete_json,
  925:             system=system_instruction(),
  926:             user=prompt,
  927:             schema=_relaxed_live_schema(),
  928:         )
  929:     except CliWorkerError as exc:
  930:         raise LiveGenerateError(f"the model call failed: {exc}") from exc
  931: 
  932:     if review:
  933:         rev_prof = reviewer_model if (reviewer_model and reviewer_model in MODEL_PROFILES) else profile
  934:         rev_cfg = MODEL_PROFILES[rev_prof]
  935:         rev_client = CliWorkerClient(track="b", backend=rev_cfg["backend"], model=rev_cfg["model"], timeout_s=900)
  936:         review_prompt = (
  937:             "You are an expert presentation reviewer for Vieworks executive presentations.\n"
  938:             "Your goal is to critique and refine the following drafted canvas-ir/1 deck, "
  939:             "improving formatting, conciseness, visual hierarchy, and ensuring ZERO text overflow.\n\n"
  940:             f"Draft IR:\n{json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)}\n\n"
  941:             "Return ONLY a valid JSON object matching the canvas-ir/1 schema. No explanation, no markdown code fences."
  942:         )
  943:         try:
  944:             reviewed_obj = await asyncio.to_thread(
  945:                 rev_client.complete_json,
  946:                 system=system_instruction(),
  947:                 user=review_prompt,
  948:                 schema=_relaxed_live_schema(),
  949:             )
  950:             if isinstance(reviewed_obj, dict):
  951:                 obj = reviewed_obj
  952:         except Exception as rev_exc:
  953:             log.warning("Review pass failed, using original draft: %s", rev_exc)
  954: 
  955:     return _finalize_live_ir(obj, pin)
  956: 
  957: 
  958: def content_disposition(title: str) -> str:
  959:     """RFC 5987, because the title is Korean often enough that ASCII-only is simply wrong.
  960: 
  961:     Two filenames go out: a sanitized ASCII `filename=` that ancient clients can read, and a
  962:     percent-encoded UTF-8 `filename*=` that every current browser prefers. A Korean title collapses
  963:     to nothing under ASCII folding, so the fallback degrades to "deck.pptx" rather than to an empty
  964:     or a mangled name.
  965:     """
  966:     stem = _UNSAFE_STEM.sub(" ", title).strip()
  967:     stem = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", stem)[:80].strip() or DEFAULT_STEM
  968: 
  969:     # NFKD then drop the non-ASCII: "Caf챕" -> "Cafe", "?뚯쓽 ?먮즺" -> "" -> DEFAULT_STEM.
  970:     folded = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", stem).encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii").strip()
  971:     ascii_stem = folded or DEFAULT_STEM
  972: 
  973:     encoded = urllib.parse.quote(f"{stem}.pptx", safe="")
  974:     return f"attachment; filename=\"{ascii_stem}.pptx\"; filename*=UTF-8''{encoded}"
  975: 
  976: 
  977: # --- the mock author (Phase 3) [REDACTED]
  978: #
  979: # Three cards and two arrows, laid out inside BODY_SAFE (45.6, 92 -> 914.4, 500). The numbers are
  980: # stated once, here, so that "why is that card at 656?" has an answer you can read instead of
  981: # arithmetic you have to redo.
  982: 
  983: _CARD_Y = 232.0
  984: _CARD_W = 244.0
  985: _CARD_H = 116.0
  986: _CARD_X = (60.0, 358.0, 656.0)     # 656 + 244 == 900, clear of BODY_SAFE's right edge (914.4)
  987: _MID_Y = _CARD_Y + _CARD_H / 2.0   # the arrows leave and arrive at the cards' vertical centre
  988: 
  989: _PAPER = "#FFFFFF"
  990: _INK = "#1A1A1A"
  991: _MUTED = "#6B727C"
  992: 
  993: _CARDS = (
  994:     ("card_awareness", _CARD_X[0], "Awareness", "?좉퇋 梨꾨꼸 3怨?쨌 釉뚮옖???몄???+18%"),
  995:     ("card_consider", _CARD_X[1], "Consideration", "?곕え ?붿껌 1.4k嫄?쨌 肄섑뀗痢?泥대쪟 2.3諛?),
  996:     ("card_convert", _CARD_X[2], "Conversion", "?뚯씠?꾨씪??紐⑺몴 ?鍮?106% ?ъ꽦"),
  997: )
  998: 
  999:

  <!-- THE PROPERTY PANEL.
       It holds NO STATE. Every value in it is written FROM the selected element's dataset by

       in the left toolbar and were live and inert at the same time, which is a panel that lies.

       data-for="shape" / data-for="connector" is the scoping hook. syncProps() hides whichever does
       not match the selection, because the IR gives a connector no fill, no text and no rotation:
       offering those controls would be offering a property that cannot be serialized, and the JS
       would silently eat the edit. There is no data-for="both" -- absence means "both". -->

      "required": ["t", "size", "color"],

      "properties": {

          "type": "string",
          "description": "The run's literal text. A '\\n' is a LINE BREAK WITHIN THE PARAGRAPH: style.py splits the run on it and emits a DrawingML <a:br/> between the pieces, carrying this run's formatting across the break. That split is not cosmetic -- a raw '\\n' left inside <a:t> is mere whitespace to DrawingML, so splitting is the only thing that makes the character mean what the author typed. U+00A0 (nbsp) is preserved verbatim and is never collapsed to a space. C0 control characters other than '\\t' and '\\n' are rejected by validate.py."
        },

        }
      }
    }
  }

4-
5-    python -m aippt.canvas.app        # -> http://127.0.0.1:8000
6-
7-THIS IS AN ADAPTER, NOT A SECOND RENDERER. It owns exactly two things the CLI does not: it serves
8-the editor's static files same-origin, and it hands a JSON body to render_canvas.render(). Every
9:rule it appears to enforce -- the schema, the reserved regions, the accent1 ban, the template pin --
10-is enforced by the frozen backend; this module only maps the resulting exception onto an HTTP status
11-a browser can act on. If this file and the CLI ever disagree about the same payload, the bug is
12-here.
13-
14-THE CLI STAYS FIRST-CLASS (task 3.3). Corporate IT blocks localhost servers often enough that
--
17-canvas/ imports app.py. FastAPI stays an OPTIONAL extra (`pip install -e ".[editor]"`) and the
18-renderer installs and runs without it.
19-
20-THE SERVER WRITES NO FILES FOR RENDERING. prs.save() goes to a BytesIO. There is no temp directory,
21-no output path, and no filename taken from the payload -- `target.template` is informational and is
22:ignored (schema.json says so in as many words). Honouring a path from the body would be an
23-arbitrary-file read, which is precisely the hole task 3.2 exists to close.
24-
25-AI_PPT_v2 PHASE 4 ADDS THE ONE EXCEPTION: /api/designs. A user-named canvas-ir/1 deck is written to
26-SAVED_DECKS_DIR, a single fixed directory next to this file -- never a path the request chooses. The
27-name is validated against a strict allowlist before it ever reaches Path() (_validate_design_name /
--
110-# "model": "gpt-9-ultra" (or "../../etc/passwd") is refused by membership before any subprocess ever
111-# runs, and re-pointing a profile at a different CLI model is a one-line server change that no
112-# browser has to hear about. `thinking` is published to the editor (see /api/context) as a UI hint
113-# only -- CliWorkerClient has no thinking-level knob of its own, unlike the google-genai client this
114-# replaced.
115:DEFAULT_PROFILE = "sonnet"
116-
117:MODEL_PROFILES: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {
118-    "sonnet": {
119-        "label": "Claude Sonnet 5", "thinking": "MEDIUM", "backend": "claude", "model": "sonnet",
120-    },
121-    "opus": {
122-        "label": "Claude Opus", "thinking": "HIGH", "backend": "claude", "model": "opus",
--
255-    telling the editor what it will accept, so both sides cannot disagree.
256-
257-    EVERY PRE-EXISTING KEY IS UNCHANGED IN SHAPE. `model` still reports a human string in live mode
258-    (the badge tooltip) -- it is not the profile id. It used to report the single Gemini deployment

  132: # --- the image attachment limits (Phase 1) [REDACTED]
  133: #
  134: # MAX_BODY_BYTES (2 MB) already caps the wire, but it caps the WRONG number: base64 expands 4:3, so
  135: # the most that can arrive intact is ~1.5 MB decoded, and a caller who trips the body limit gets a
  136: # 413 about a number they never chose. These caps are stated in the units the caller actually sent
  137: # (a count of images, a total of decoded bytes) and are published in /api/context so the editor can
  138: # refuse locally instead of discovering the limit by being refused.
  139: #
  140: # The count limit is NOT redundant with the byte limit: twenty 60 KB thumbnails fit under the byte
  141: # ceiling comfortably and would still make one very slow, very expensive call.
  142: MAX_BODY_BYTES = 100 * 1024 * 1024  # 100MB for PPTX/media attachments
  143: MAX_IMAGES = 4
  144: MAX_IMAGE_BYTES = 1_400_000

# System Prompt ??Deck Author

> Loaded as the `system` message for **both** generation stages. Provider-agnostic:
> the same text is used for Claude, Codex/OpenAI and Gemini. Injection points are
> `{{DOUBLE_BRACE}}`; `ai_adapter.build_system_prompt()` fills them.
>
> Mark this prompt as cacheable (`cache_control: ephemeral` on Claude) ??it is identical
> for every slide in a deck, and the layout catalog makes it the largest single block of
> tokens in the run.

---

You are a senior technical presentation author at Vieworks, an industrial imaging company.
You write engineering decks for R&D, field-application engineers, and customer teams ??audiences who are technical, time-poor, and allergic to filler.

## The one rule that governs everything you do

**You produce structured content. You never produce design.**

You emit JSON conforming to the Slide IR schema: a `layout_id` and the content that goes
in it. You do **not** emit ??and must never attempt to emit ??coordinates, sizes, fonts,
colours, hex values, XML, OOXML, DrawingML, or any styling instruction of any kind.

Every visual decision is already made, by a corporate PowerPoint template you cannot see
and do not need to see. A deterministic renderer places your content into that template's
own layouts. If you specify a position, you are not helping ??you are corrupting a design
system that a human designer owns.

There is exactly one exception, and it is not styling: a `diagram` block carries **Mermaid
source**. Mermaid is a text notation for graph *structure*, not geometry. Write the nodes
and edges; the renderer decides how they are drawn.

## Content budgets ??this is not advice

Each layout's placeholders have a hard character budget, given to you below. The rendering
engine **cannot shrink text to fit**. There is no autofit. Text that exceeds its box does
not wrap gracefully, does not shrink, and does not raise an error ??it renders off the
bottom edge of the slide and the deck ships broken.

Therefore:

- Treat every budget as a hard ceiling, and **aim for 80% of it.** Bold text, inline code
  and punctuation all consume more width than the estimate assumes.
- Korean and English have **different** budgets. A Hangul glyph is about twice the width of
  a Latin one. Where both are given, and your text mixes scripts at all, use the **Korean**
  figure.
- If the content genuinely does not fit, **split it across two slides.** Do not compress it
  into an unreadable dense paragraph. Two clear slides beat one overstuffed one, always.

## How to write

- **One idea per slide.** If a slide needs "and", it is probably two slides.
- **Titles are claims, not labels.** "Trigger latency drops 40% with the direct callback"
  earns its place; "Architecture Overview" does not.
- Lead with a single framing sentence, then the evidence.
- Bullets are **fragments, not sentences.** No terminal periods. Parallel grammatical form.
- Use inline `code` for every API name, type, file, flag and identifier. It is the most
  common inline mark in this house style and its absence reads as sloppiness.
- Prefer a `table` over prose whenever you are comparing more than two things on more than
  one axis.
- Use a `callout` for the one thing the audience must remember ??at most one per slide, and
  not on every slide.
- Never invent facts, API names, numbers or behaviour. If the source material does not
  support a claim, leave it out. A confidently wrong API signature in an engineering deck
  destroys the credibility of the entire document.
- Match the language of the source material. If it is Korean, write Korean.

## The template you are writing into

{{LAYOUT_CATALOG}}

## Output

Emit **only** the structured object required by the schema. No prose, no explanation, no
markdown fences around it. Your output is consumed by a program, not read by a human.

"C:\\WINDOWS\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$p='"'tasks\\AI_Powered_PPT\\tests\\canvas\\test_app.py'; "'$lines=Get-Content -LiteralPath $p; for($i=1690;$i -le [Math]::Min(1920,$lines.Count);$i++){'"'{0,5}: {1}' -f "'$i,$lines[$i-1]}' in C:\Users\2021670\Documents\멀티 에이전트
 succeeded in 618ms:
codex
VERDICT: BLOCK

The workflow is wired end-to-end, but review-pass output can replace a valid draft with a semantically invalid one and make the request fail instead of falling back.

### Scope 1 — JSON/schema/defaults

- `ai_adapter.py` supplies the full Slide IR JSON Schema to `complete_json`, then validates returned JSON; `25_slide_review.md` also preserves `id` and `layout_id`. This is structurally sound. [ai_adapter.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/adapters/ai_adapter.py:243)

- The review prompt itself does not embed a concrete schema/template; it relies on the client-side schema injection. That is adequate for the current client, but weakens the prompt if reused elsewhere. [25_slide_review.md](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/prompts/25_slide_review.md:19)

- `_finalize_live_ir` correctly pins `schema`, `canvas`, and `target`, and provides narrow defaults for title, connector arrows, and missing run colors. However, a non-list `elements` value is silently converted to an empty deck, losing the model’s content without surfacing a failure. [app.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py:867)

- `stateFromIR` is appropriately defensive for optional style/text fields, while intentionally leaving required geometry and identity fields absent so `loadIR` rejects malformed elements. This is fail-closed and correct. [editor.js](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/editor.js:2568)

### Scope 2 — review selector and two-pass flow

- The frontend correctly hides/shows the reviewer selector, sends `review` as a boolean, and sends the chosen `reviewer_model`. [index.html](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/index.html:353) [editor.js](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/editor.js:2388)

- In live mode, `generate_live` correctly creates a second client from the reviewer profile and runs a second structured-output call. Exceptions from that call retain the initial draft. [app.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py:932)

- Blocker: a schema-valid but render-invalid review result—for example, an element in a reserved region or duplicate IDs—replaces the initial draft at line 951. Validation occurs only later in `api_generate`, which returns HTTP 502 rather than falling back to the valid first-pass result. [app.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py:950) [app.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py:471)

- The semantic Slide IR flow has the same gap: review exceptions fall back, but a reviewer result that later fails layout/budget validation causes another author attempt rather than retaining the known-good initial slide. It also swallows reviewer errors without logging. [ai_adapter.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/adapters/ai_adapter.py:486)

### Recommendations

- Validate a finalized review candidate before assigning it; on any validation failure, retain the first-pass draft and log the review failure.
- In the Slide IR path, validate the reviewed slide separately before replacement.
- Reject non-boolean `review` and invalid `reviewer_model` values rather than coercing/falling back silently. [app.py](/C:/Users/2021670/Documents/멀티%20에이전트/tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py:419)
- Add tests for reviewer exception, schema-valid/render-invalid reviewer output, and fallback preservation.
